<note>The <term>Ratio Disciplinae</term> (System of Discipline) was a vital document for the <term>Unitas Fratrum</term> (Unity of the Brethren), a proto-Protestant group from the Kingdom of Bohemia. This 1660 edition, published in Amsterdam, was edited by Jan Amos Comenius (Komenský), the famous educator and last bishop of the original Unity. As the group faced extinction in their homeland during the Counter-Reformation, Comenius published this to preserve their heritage and provide a model of church order for future generations.</note>

<summary>This is the title page for the 1660 Amsterdam edition of the Bohemian Brethren's "System of Discipline," a foundational text for their communal and spiritual life. It includes a printer's device featuring Saint Christopher and administrative stamps from the Reformed Church in Vienna.</summary>

# TO GREAT BRITAIN'S
## GREAT MONARCH,
# CHARLES II,
## DEFENDER OF THE FAITH,

->Every prosperity from Heaven.<- <note>original Latin: "Omnem Cælitùs prosperitatem." A formal blessing wishing the King divine favor.</note>

### Most Serene King,

Just as nothing in human affairs is blessed in every respect, so too that Great Happiness of your Britain—which was restored to her by your return to your ancestral kingdoms (by the favor of the Most High <note>The text ends mid-sentence here; the author is beginning to describe the Restoration of 1660 as a divinely ordained event. "Altissimi" (the Most High) refers to God.</note>

---

<summary>This page contains the formal dedication of the book to King Charles II of England, celebrating his 1660 restoration to the throne. The author frames the King's return as a divinely sanctioned event bringing happiness to Britain.</summary>
<keywords>Charles II, Restoration, Great Britain, dedication, monarchy, divine providence, Bohemian Brethren</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, completing the thought regarding the King’s restoration to his kingdoms through divine intervention.</meta>

(and the provision of His right hand), now suffers a certain dimness due to the intervention of a specific little cloud. Yet there is hope that, through the mercy of God and through your own moderation—as a wise and **Peaceful Solomon** <note>original: "Pacifici Salomonis." A common contemporary comparison for King Charles II, likening him to the biblical King Solomon to emphasize his role as a bringer of peace and wisdom after the English Civil Wars.</note>—this cloud shall be driven away.

By this "little cloud," I mean the situation of the Church of Christ <note>original: "Ecclesiæ Christi." Here referring specifically to the Church of England.</note> among your people, which is most flourishing by the grace of God. To this Church, which is otherwise in harmony, a single question—vexingly debated for an entire century now—regarding *The Best Form of Church Government* <note>original: "De Optima Ecclesiastici Regiminis Forma." This refers to the ongoing "Smectymnuan" and "Hooker-style" debates between those favoring rule by bishops (Episcopacy) and those favoring elders (Presbyterianism).</note>, still threatens some degree of division. This will continue unless, through moderate counsels, both the Prelates <note>original: "Præfules." High-ranking church leaders or bishops.</note> of the Churches themselves reach an agreement among one another, and You—who preside in God's place over the affairs of your realm—along with the Great [Angel] of Great Counsel <note>original: "Magno Magni Consili." Likely referring to the "Angel of Great Counsel" (Christ), based on Isaiah 9:6 in the Septuagint/Vulgate tradition, suggesting the King should rule with divine guidance.</note>...

<summary>The author identifies a "little cloud" of religious division regarding church government that threatens the peace of the newly restored England. He appeals to King Charles II to use Solomonic wisdom to reconcile the differing factions of the Church.</summary>

<keywords>Charles II, Church of England, Church Government, Solomon, Prelates, moderation, religious unity, Restoration</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the thought from the previous page, completing the sentence regarding the King ruling alongside the "Great Council" of God and his own advisors.</meta>

...and with your **Council** <note>original: "Senatu." In this context, it refers to the Parliament or the King's Privy Council advising on the settlement of the Church.</note>, may you prudently arrange all things toward peace.

We foreigners, out of our deep respect for your Church (which the **Eternal Groom** <note>original: "æternus Sponsus." A theological reference to Jesus Christ, who in biblical tradition is the "husband" of the Church.</note> has adorned for himself with the most beautiful gifts above many others), can wish for nothing but good things. Yet we also feel ourselves bound to the practical duties of the **Communion of Saints** <note>original: "Communionis sanctorum." This refers to the spiritual bond between all Christians; the author uses it to justify his involvement in English affairs as a matter of shared faith.</note>, if indeed anything within our power might contribute to it. Truly, from me (who am the least in the **House of God** <note>original: "Domo Dei." A standard expression of humility, referring to the author's position within the global Christian community.</note>, though not the last among those interceding for you before God), a certain request has been made by several distinguished men among you, from both parties: *namely, a communication regarding the Governance of our Churches, such as it was*

<meta>catchword: while (original: "dum")</meta>

<summary>The author expresses his foreign perspective and prayers for the English Church, noting that leaders from both the Episcopalian and Presbyterian factions have asked him to share how churches are governed in his own land.</summary>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, where the author was explaining his motivation for writing a description of his own church's governance at the request of English church leaders.</meta>

...while it flourished <note>original: "dum fuit." Refers to the previous or established state of the author's church governance.</note>, if perhaps anything might be drawn from it that serves the cause of Peace and spiritual growth <note>original: "ædificationi." A theological term for the "building up" or strengthening of the church community.</note>.

I have done so, I have shared it, and now I send it! It is dedicated to your British Church <note>original: "Britannicæ Ecclesiæ."</note>. Furthermore, since it is not proper for private individuals to attempt matters that concern the public welfare <note>original: "publicam... salutem." Refers to the common good or the stability of the state and church.</note>: however, Great **God** has appointed **You**, **Great King**, as the Head of the Nations of Your Realm, the Arbiter of Disputes, and the Defender of His Church and the Faith <note>original: "Fidei Defensorem." A traditional title of the English monarch, originally granted to Henry VIII and retained by his successors.</note>, this little work, such as it is <note>original: "Opellæ." A humble diminutive meaning "a tiny piece of work."</note>, I lay before the throne <note>original: "pulvinar." Literally "cushion," used here as a respectful term for the royal presence.</note> of Your Majesty: asking most humbly on my own behalf that, for those who are debating the aforementioned Controversy <note>original: "Controversiam." Likely referring to the ongoing 17th-century disputes over church government—specifically between those favoring bishops (Episcopalians) and those favoring elders (Presbyterians).</note> among you, this little treatise might be provided for their review...

<summary>The author presents his description of foreign church governance to the King of England, arguing that while private citizens should not interfere in public affairs, the King has the divine authority to use such resources to settle religious disputes.</summary>
<keywords>King of Great Britain, Defender of the Faith, church controversy, religious peace, ecclesiastical governance, royal authority</keywords>

...while it flourished, and if it please you to recommend that whatever might be selected from here to diminish hatred and truly increase goodwill <note>original: "favores." In this context, it refers to mutual support or favorable relations between religious factions.</note> should be considered. In the public name of the Churches, and indeed in the name of Christ, I pray that Your Majesty <term>Majestas Vestra</term> <note>A formal address to the monarch, emphasizing their divinely appointed role.</note> does not allow this dispute—the only one remaining in <gloss>those</gloss> Your churches—to <gloss>that</gloss> erupt <gloss>into a</gloss> new schism <term>schisma</term> <note>A formal split or division within the church. The author likely refers to the growing divide between the Anglican establishment and the Puritans or Presbyterians.</note>! For the glory of God! For the safety <note>original: "salutem." Referring here to the health and well-being of the church as an institution.</note> of the Church! For the peace of Your Kingdoms and Your own peace! For a good example to the Churches of the entire world! In this way, the **King of Kings** <note>A biblical title for God, placing the earthly monarch's power under divine authority.</note> shall establish Your Royal Throne and cause You, along with **Your House** and **Your Kingdoms**, to flourish and endure.

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page's dedication to King Charles II, Comenius now turns his address directly to the institutional Church of England. The catchword "3. Vel" suggests the continuation of his points on the following page.</meta>

# TO THE CHURCH OF ENGLAND,
### tossed until now by various storms, but now looking toward the harbor of her own tranquility, <note>Comenius writes this during the Restoration of the English monarchy (c. 1660), after the Church of England had been suppressed during the English Civil War and the Protectorate of Oliver Cromwell.</note> Greetings in the Lord.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'T' featuring floral and foliate scrollwork patterns within a square border.</image-desc>

The turbulent state of affairs, such as can now be seen among nations everywhere, is something sad in itself; nevertheless, it seems to announce the hope of some most beautiful turning point <note>original: *conversionis* — a turning, revolution, or transformation</note> to those who do not disdain to look attentively at the practice of God’s works throughout the ages.

2. For indeed, just as from the beginning of all things God brought forth light from darkness and the most orderly appearance of things from a confused <term>chaos (chao)</term>: so too in the course of His governance, whatever disturbances He has ever permitted to happen to the human race, He has always turned them toward good: whether at least by making the <term>godly (pios)</term> more practiced <note>original: *exercitatiores* — meaning more disciplined, tested, or strengthened through trial</note>, or by leading even the <term>ungodly (impios)</term> occasionally from the darkness of ignorance to a participation in the light. *So that the Church might always take its growth through its very ruins, either into something greater or into something better.*

<summary>Comenius addresses the Church of England during the Restoration, offering a providential view of history where God uses periods of "chaos" and "darkness" to refine the faithful and expand the Church.</summary>
<keywords>Church of England, Restoration, Providence, divine order, chaos, religious peace, Johan-Amos Comenius</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 3 of the dedication. Comenius is now providing a series of historical examples from the Hebrew Bible to illustrate his point that God uses periods of crisis and "ruin" to bring about greater manifestations of His power and grace.</meta>

3. Or, if one considers the various fortunes of the *Israelite People*, it will become clear that all their punishments, captivities, and migrations from place to place were nothing other than ever-new opportunities for the works of God to be revealed.

4. Who does not see that *Abraham*, the Father of believers himself, was worn down by such great wanderings, dangers, and promises that for a long time seemed empty, precisely so that his *Faith*, having been thus tested, might learn to lean on hope against hope? <note>original: *in spem contra spem* — a reference to Romans 4:18, describing Abraham's faith despite his old age.</note> And so that the Church might have a most illustrious example of heroic Faith that overcomes all worldly things.

5. God Himself testifies (Exodus 9:16) that his descendants were crushed by *Egyptian* slavery in such a horrific manner *so that there might be an opportunity to show the power of God in Pharaoh, and that through this, the name of God might be proclaimed across the whole Earth.*

6. What could seem more wretched than the fact that the People of God, defeated by their *Philistine* enemies, had lost the very symbol of the divine presence and the hope of heavenly help: the most holy *Ark* of the Covenant? And that this Ark, captured by enemies and led away in triumph, was brought into the shrine of *Dagon* <note>Dagon was the primary deity of the Philistines, often depicted as a fish-god.</note>, and thus—
<note>original: *sic* — this is the catchword, indicating the start of the next page.</note>

<summary>Comenius uses the biblical examples of Abraham, the Exodus from Egypt, and the capture of the Ark of the Covenant to argue that the Church's periods of suffering are intentional divine tests meant to display God's power. He frames the recent struggles of the Church of England within this providential history of "hope against hope."</summary>
<keywords>Providence, Abraham, Faith, Exodus, Pharaoh, Philistines, Ark of the Covenant, Dagon, Church history, resilience</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 4, where Comenius was describing the capture of the Ark of the Covenant by the Philistines and its placement in the temple of the idol Dagon.</meta>

->Address<-
A 3
<meta>catchword: savage-</meta>

—was thus made a mockery by that diabolical Idol and its idolaters? And yet, how beautifully did this most terrible event serve to illustrate the glory of the true God and to expose the vanity of idols? <note>Referring to the biblical account in 1 Samuel 5, where the idol Dagon repeatedly fell prostrate before the captured Ark.</note>

7. It was a sorrowful thing for the people of Judah—and among them the lineage of David (to whom such great promises had been made for eternity) and many holy men—to be led away into the *Babylonian captivity*. And yet, there, *Ezekiel* was raised up as a Prophet; with what great revelations did he illuminate the Church? Truly, *Daniel*, through his miraculous deeds and sufferings in that same place, led so many kings and nations to the knowledge of the true God. This is evident from his book: Chapter 2, verses 4, 6, and 47; Chapter 4, verses 1, 2, and 3; and Chapter 6, verse 26, etc.

8. To what extremes did the plots of *Haman against the scattered people of God, the Jews*, not reach? In a single day, they were all to be wiped out. And yet, how much salvation, joy, and honor did this very savage crime bring to those people? And what an example of divine *Providence*—which always stands watch for its own—did it provide for all posterity? The Book of Esther shows this. <note>Haman was a high official in the Persian Empire who plotted the genocide of the Jews, a plot foiled by Queen Esther.</note>

9. What shall I say of the *Antiochan oppression against that same people* <note>original: *Antiochæa* — referring to the persecutions of the Jewish people by the Seleucid king Antiochus IV Epiphanes, which led to the Maccabean Revolt.</note>

<summary>Comenius continues his historical survey, arguing that the Babylonian captivity and the plots of Haman were actually divine instruments used to reveal God's power through prophets like Daniel and Ezekiel and the providence shown in the Book of Esther.</summary>
<keywords>Babylonian Captivity, Ezekiel, Daniel, Haman, Book of Esther, Providence, Antiochus Epiphanes, Jewish history, biblical typology</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 5, where the author was discussing the "savage cruelty" of the Antiochan oppression against the Jewish people.</meta>

### Dedication
A
<meta>catchword: rising-</meta>

—savage cruelty? <note>original: *Sævitia* — This completes the sentence from the previous page, referring to the persecutions under Antiochus IV.</note> Has it not produced so many glorious Martyrs? Has it not produced the **Maccabees**, those men of virtue, along with so many comforting examples for the **Church** of the **Providence** of the Almighty Divine (who directs all things according to His own will)? <note>The Maccabees were Jewish leaders who led a revolt against the Seleucid Empire to defend their religious freedom; their story is found in the deuterocanonical Books of the Maccabees.</note>

10. The *dispersion of the Apostolic Church from Jerusalem* was a sad event, and yet it was nothing else but the spreading of the **Gospel** to other nations (Acts 8, v. 4, etc.). And how greatly did John’s *exile on Patmos* enrich the Church with a vast prophetic treasure! <note>The Apostle John was traditionally exiled to the island of Patmos, where he is said to have received the revelations that form the Book of Revelation.</note>

11. Likewise, however many hostilities and persecutions from without, or contradictions, quarrels, **heresies**, or **schisms** from within, have ever emerged in the Church; just as many occasions have always arisen for producing most glorious ranks of martyrs, or for composing the most noble writings. By these very means, the wicked are confounded by the constancy of the saints, and the godly are more thoroughly bathed in the light of the more radiant **Truth**.

12. Finally, regarding the *last times of the world* (which are now), although the highest Prophet himself, our Lord, foretold terrible things—namely, *that it would come to pass that we should hear of wars and rumors of wars* (Matthew 24, v. 6). In the rising—

<summary>The author argues that historical persecutions and internal conflicts, from the Maccabees to the Apostles, have ultimately served to spread the Gospel and strengthen the Church's faith. He frames contemporary wars as the fulfillment of biblical prophecy regarding the end times.</summary>
<keywords>Martyrs, Maccabees, Divine Providence, Apostolic Church, Gospel, Exile on Patmos, Heresy, Truth, End Times, Matthew 24</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s commentary on the apocalyptic signs mentioned in Matthew 24, following the previous page's discussion of wars and rumors of wars.</meta>

->address<-

A 4
<meta>catchword: our</meta>

—nation rising against nation, and kingdom against kingdom, and there will be famines and earthquakes; yet He added, *See that you are not troubled!* <note>original: *Videte ne turbemini!* — A direct quote from Matthew 24:6, where Jesus encourages His followers during times of crisis.</note> For all these things must happen: but the end is not yet (v. 8). *Because the Gospel of the kingdom will first be preached in the whole world, as a testimony to all nations: and then the end will come* (v. 14).

13. Therefore, let nations now also clash before our eyes (by the force of fate, though to the greatest grief of the godly), let kingdoms and empires rush against one another, let the earth itself tremble with horror, and let cities, temples, schools, and finally all things private and public be filled with ruins: yet that same God (who in the beginning brought forth the most beautiful world from the foulest **chaos** <note>original: *chao* — The primal state of disorder before the creation of the universe.</note>, and who for the sake of His **church** <note>original: *Ecclesia*</note> preserves it powerfully until now and governs it wisely) will know how to draw out something better from even these **upheavals** <note>original: *concussionibus* — literally "shakings" or "shocks," often used for political or social turmoil.</note> than any of us can imagine. Namely, so that (just as He promised) the **Gospel** <note>The "Good News" of the Christian faith.</note> might finally pass from the harshly disciplined Christians even to the remaining nations of the world. So that (as once before) *our stumble might become the wealth of the world, and our loss* <note>original: *offensa nostra... diminutio nostra* — This is a paraphrase of Romans 11:12, suggesting that the temporary setbacks or "stumbles" of one group serve to spread spiritual wealth to others.</note>

<summary>The author interprets global turmoil and the destruction of institutions as part of a divine plan to spread the Gospel to all nations. He argues that just as God created order from chaos, He will bring a greater good out of the current "upheavals" facing the Church.</summary>

<keywords>Gospel, Providence, Chaos, Church, Nations, Kingdom, Upheavals, Matthew 24, Romans 11</keywords>

# Dedication

<meta>catchword: restoration</meta>

*...our loss becomes the riches of the Nations* (Rom. 11:12). <note>original: *nostra divitiæ Gentium* — The author is completing the thought from the previous page, suggesting that the suffering and dispersal of his specific church might lead to the spiritual enrichment of the wider world.</note>

14. This consideration of such an admirable plan of eternal **Providence** <note>The protective care of God as a spiritual power.</note> soothes my pains regarding the ruin of the **Church of my Nation** <note>The *Unitas Fratrum* (Unity of the Brethren), a pre-Reformation Protestant church in the Czech lands.</note>, the laws for governing which—while it was in its proper state—are presented here described. But I, the last among the last—alas!—the **Chief Overseer** <note>original: *Antistes* — A term for a high-ranking church leader or bishop.</note>, am closing the door behind me (in your sight, O Churches!). And so that I may close it honorably, I have decided to justify the divine judgments against us with this little meditation, and to provide myself—along with the remnants of my people who still remain, and whoever wishes to beware of losses by following our example—with some comfort and some warnings.

15. For the flood of a most atrocious **Persecution** <note>The systematic suppression of Protestants in Bohemia and Moravia during and after the Thirty Years' War (1618–1648).</note> lasting forty years has so destroyed us that in all of **Bohemia** and **Moravia** we do not have even one Church remaining, except for a few remnants of the flock scattered through neighboring kingdoms. Furthermore, because of the long duration of our **exile**, it has happened that almost all the **Pastors** of the Churches—certainly all without exception: the **Bishops**, **Superintendents**, **Deans**, and **Archdeacons**—have ceased to be among the living, down to me alone: who, for the sake of the **restoration**... <note>original: *restitutionis* — The author likely intended to discuss the hope of restoring the church order, but the sentence is completed on the following page.</note>

<summary>The author, identifying as the last surviving bishop of the Bohemian Brethren, reflects on the total destruction of his church in its homeland after forty years of war and persecution. He presents this record of church laws as a final legacy for those remaining in exile.</summary>
<keywords>John Amos Comenius, Bohemia, Moravia, Thirty Years' War, Unitas Fratrum, religious exile, church governance, Providence, persecution</keywords>

# Address

A 5
<meta>catchword: to</meta>

...hope of **restoration** <note>original: *restorationis* — The previous page ended with "restora-". The author refers to the hope of rebuilding the church.</note> in the **Year 1632** (by the will and votes of the **Church gathered in Synod** <note>A formal meeting of church leaders to decide on doctrine and administration.</note>), I was chosen while the **Overseers** <note>original: *Antistites* — High-ranking leaders or bishops.</note> were still alive. Now, I alone survive with a few remaining brother **Ministers** (and a single colleague from the **Polish nation**), for as long as it pleases the Author of Life. For although after the final dispersion from **Poland**, four years ago, I labored earnestly so that I would not be the only and last one, it did not please God to grant success to my plans; meanwhile, those among the primary leaders who could have succeeded me have also been taken from human affairs <note>An idiom meaning they have died.</note>.

16. However, I would not want these things to be taken as if I, because of my solitude and my coming departure from here, fear or predict an end for my Church among my people. *I know that the **Church** founded upon the rock of eternity does not perish—namely, the **Universal** <note>original: *Catholicam* — Used here in the literal sense of "universal" or "whole," referring to the entire body of Christian believers rather than the Roman Catholic denomination specifically.</note> Church; yet examples testify that **particular Churches** are sometimes overturned by the hand of an angry God (so that at His good pleasure others may be planted, or the same one in a different way).* Indeed, God Himself says: *The end has come for my people **Israel**; I will no longer spare them* (Amos 8:2). And with the destruction already completed...

# Dedication

<meta>catchword: 18. Con-</meta>

...to Baruch: *“You said, ‘Woe to wretched me, for the Lord has added sorrow to my sorrow!’ Thus says the Lord: ‘What I have built I destroy, what I have planted I uproot,’”* etc. (Jeremiah 45:3, 4). Finally, the Church itself, now overturned, says of itself: *“Our end has approached, our days are fulfilled, our end has come”* (Lamentations 4:18 <note>original: *Thren.* — Short for *Threni*, the Latin name for the Book of Lamentations.</note>).

17. Nor should it be thought that **Elijah** sinned because, complaining that he alone survived, he feared for the Church; in this very solitude and anxiety of his, he was lifted up by divine comfort and instructed by God’s counsel regarding a successor to be appointed in his place (1 Kings 19:14, etc. <note>original: *1. Reg.* — Referring to the First Book of Kings.</note>). Expecting this same thing from that same eternal goodness with full confidence—even if God should nevertheless command me to depart from here <note>A common euphemism for death.</note> without any present comfort—I will say, along with the last of the seven **Maccabean** brothers, those martyrs: *“In me and in my brothers let the wrath of the Almighty be stayed, which has been brought upon our race because of our sins”* (2 Maccabees 7:38). And if I do not obtain this prayer, that the wrath of God might end with me, and our **Mother** <note>The author likens his Church to the mother of the seven Maccabean martyrs, who, according to the biblical account, died after her sons.</note> must also finally die (as in verse 41), what shall I do?

<meta>This page continues the author's reflection on his role as perhaps the last leader of his church. ->Address<-

<meta>catchword: ni</meta>

18. *It has become an accepted **custom of nations** <note>original: *Consvetudine gentium*</note> that those who die without an heir should transfer their goods to others, whomever they may be. This has gone so far that certain Kings, the last of their line, designated the **Roman People** as the heir to their kingdoms (as did **Attalus** of Pergamum, and **Ptolemy** of Cyrene, and others <note>Attalus III and Ptolemy Apion were historical kings who famously willed their kingdoms to the Roman Republic to prevent civil war or annexation by rivals.</note>). Why, then, should I not also be allowed to provide for the things entrusted to me in some way, if indeed, according to God’s will, I must depart without a successor?*

19. *But to whom shall I transfer our goods? Or what goods, now that everything has already been lost? By God’s gift, there still remains that which may be bequeathed by **testament**; and there is no lack of those to whom it may be bequeathed—friends and enemies! To our enemies, let us bequeath those things which could be snatched away, or may be in the future: external goods, Temples, Fields, Schools, and so on. Finally, our very life which still remains to us, if it so pleases God, the judge of all things; just as it pleased **Christ**, while hanging on the cross, that his clothes be divided among the soldiers and his very life be taken away.*

20. *But to you, **Friends** (of the same eternal...* <note>The sentence remains unfinished as it continues onto the next page.</note>

### Dedication

->Address<-

<meta>catchword: ni</meta>

...of the same eternal Teacher, following his example) we **commend better treasures: our very beloved Mother, the Church.** <note>original: *Ecclesiam*</note> From this point on, take up the care of her in our stead: whether God deigns to revive her among us, or whether He chooses to bring her back to life elsewhere, though she has died here at home. Truly, you ought to love her even in death, she who, while living, led the way for you through examples of **Faith and Patience** <note>original: *Fidei & Patientiae*</note> even into the third century. <note>The author is likely referring to the three-hundred-year history of his specific religious tradition, the Unity of the Brethren, which faced extinction during the Counter-Reformation.</note>

21. But how can I commend her now that she is vanishing? *What care can be given to a thing that no longer exists?*

**Response:** For deceased friends, it is customary to erect sepulchral **monuments** <note>original: *monumenta*</note>, so that their memory does not easily fade away. Moreover, for those who once shone with virtues, **portrayals of those very Virtues are shaped as a mirror for posterity.** <note>The author suggests that his writings serve as a "mirror" or a moral guide for future generations to reflect upon and imitate.</note> Finally, when God Himself took away and overthrew the Land, the City, and the Temple from a people ungrateful for His benefits, **He nevertheless willed that the foundations of the Altar be left in their place, upon which posterity (having returned to themselves and to God) might rebuild.** (Ezra 3:3) <note>original: *Altaris fundamenta*</note> Therefore, if by the gift of God there remains to us anything true, anything honorable, anything just, any—

### Address

<meta>catchword: lo-</meta>

...whatever is pure, whatever is lovely and of good report, and if there be any virtue, and if there be any praise <note>original: *quidqvam purum, quidqvam amabile & boni nominis, & si qva virtus, & si qva laus* — a direct allusion to Philippians 4:8</note>, was present (as wise and pious men have judged, which will be revealed in its proper place), we must provide that these things do not perish along with us. By placing this **Trust** <note>original: *Deposito*; a legal or sacred "deposit" left in someone's care</note> of ours in your hands, that provision is being made.

22. Indeed, I thought this had already been accomplished when I recently presented the history by *Jan Lasitius, a Polish Nobleman, containing memorable accounts of the Ecclesiastical Discipline, Customs, and Institutions of the Bohemian Brethren* <note>Jan Łasicki (1534–1602) was a Polish historian; Comenius edited and published his work on the Brethren in 1660</note> (to use his own words), which was recently reprinted in Amsterdam. <meta>catchword: Uni-</meta>

...not accustomed to speaking of themselves, had been published; and that this fuller and more exquisite work, prepared by an outsider who was an admirer of our <term>Order</term> <note>original: *Ordinis*; here referring to the *Ratio Disciplinae* or the structured way of life and governance of the Bohemian Brethren</note>, provided more abundant information on these matters. Nevertheless, my friends persisted, and unanimously urged a new edition of that earlier little work, so that I eventually promised I would satisfy this demand as well.

23. Therefore, I am now fulfilling my promise, and behold, I send what I was asked for in multiple copies! I have changed nothing in the substance of the matter, as the writing is not my own, but the public record of the Church. However, where it seemed useful to express certain things more specifically—to better understand the internal bonds of our Order and to observe its practice more easily—I have added certain Notes (explaining more fully what was stated concisely).

24. I commend you to the Spirit of grace: who, with that Apostolic rule, *Let all things be done decently and in ORDER* <note>original: *Omnia decenter & ORDINE fiant*; quoting 1 Corinthians 14:40</note>, inscribed on your heart, may now unite, incorporate, and solidify you all (*the entire Body with its glorious Head, and all the Members of the Political and mystical Body among themselves*) with the indissoluble bond of Faith, Love, and eternal Harmony! May it be so, that just as Britain recently appeared to the world as a sad theater of dissensions <note>A reference to the turmoil of the English Civil Wars (1642–1651)</note>, it may now become a living exemplar of Harmony. May you act with courage in God, and successfully trample upon your enemies, under your own *Great* Constantine <note>Constantine the Great was proclaimed Emperor in Britain (York) and defeated his rivals Maxentius and Licinius; the author here uses him as a symbol for a strong leader (likely Charles II, given the timing of Comenius's later works) who restores religious and civil peace</note>, who has been not called away from Britain, but recalled to Britain, against the modern-day Maxentiuses and Liciniuses. <meta>This page begins the main historical narrative following the dedication. Comenius is establishing the ancient lineage of the Slavonic Church to demonstrate that the Bohemian Reformation was not a "new" invention, but a return to Apostolic roots.</meta>

<page-num>1</page-num>
<sig>A</sig>

<image-desc>An ornate woodcut headpiece featuring symmetrical floral scrolls, foliage, and two bird-like figures or griffins facing outward at the top corners.</image-desc>

# THE SLAVONIC CHURCH
### *A brief History of the Church founded by the Apostles themselves, spread by Jerome, Cyril, and Methodius, rooted most especially among the Bohemian people, and reaching its pinnacle in the Unity of the Bohemian Brethren.* <note>The "Unity of the Brethren" (Latin: *Unitas Fratrum*) was the proto-Protestant group to which Comenius belonged, today known as the Moravian Church.</note>

---

<image-desc>A decorative drop cap 'C' containing a small illustration of a seated figure, possibly a saint or scholar, within the curve of the letter.</image-desc>

Worthy of consideration, pious reader, are those words of the eternal Bridegroom, Christ, spoken as if in wonder concerning His Bride, the Church: *Who is she that rises like the* **DAWN**? *fair as the* **MOON**? *pure as the* **SUN**? *as formidable as an* **ARMY SET IN BATTLE ARRAY**? <margin>(Song of Solomon 6:9)</margin> <note>original: *Cant. 6: v. 9*; in modern Bibles, this is usually verse 10.</note> These words, whether they are taken in a divided sense or joined together, offer the most beautiful lessons.

**2** If we take them in a divided sense—regarding the Universal Church as it is divided into particular parts—it is clear that Churches can and should be distinguished and viewed by us in the same way they appear in the eyes of God and in the truth of the matter. That is to say: some are new, only just rising, whose light is like the **Dawn**; others are more advanced, receiving the increase of their brilliance like the **Moon**; others exult in the full light of Faith, Love, and the hope of eternal mercy, like the **Sun**; and finally, some, besides their internal brightness at home, spread terror outward from themselves like a **well-ordered army**, striking horror into their enemies by their very battle line and banners.

<summary>Comenius begins his history by using the poetic imagery of the Song of Solomon to describe the different stages of a Church's development, from its early "dawn" to its disciplined "army-like" maturity.</summary>

<keywords>Slavonic Church, Church History, St. Jerome, Cyril and Methodius, Unity of the Brethren, Song of Solomon, Ecclesiology</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of the Church's history using images from the Song of Solomon. Having established that the Church can be viewed in stages, Comenius now maps those stages onto specific historical eras: from the Apostolic "Dawn" to the "Moonlight" of the era of heresies, the "Sunlight" of the Reformation, and the final "Battle Array" of a unified Church.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>
<header>Of the Church</header>
<meta>catchword: CE (completing "PEACE")</meta>

**3.** If we take these words in a joined sense—referring to the entire, one Universal Church <note>original: *Catholica Ecclesia*; Comenius uses "Catholic" in its literal sense of "universal" or "whole."</note> (just as Christ speaks in the preceding verse: *My dove, my perfect one, is the only one* <note>Song of Solomon 6:9</note>)—it is beyond doubt that its various ages and stages are being hinted at. Indeed, the light of the newly rising Church (under the Apostles) was truly the **Dawn**. The faithful exulted in this especially, because *the night was far spent, the day was at hand, and it was time to wake from sleep* (Romans 13:11–12; 2 Peter 1:19). In that era, Theology was not yet as highly refined as it later became; nor did there exist such a clear and distinct knowledge of the mysteries of faith as was developed after the delusions of heretics tested the wisdom of the Church. It was enough for those early believers *to cast off the works of darkness, and put on the armor of light, and to walk honestly as in the day* (Romans 13:13), as their most fervent piety, proved by their martyrdoms, clearly shows.

**4.** There followed the age of the **Moon**, of waxing and waning light, during the time of the Heretics. The Church’s conflict with them met with varying success—at times Truth prevailed over errors, and at other times errors prevailed over Truth—until the Church even suffered an "Eclipse" under the Antichrist. <note>In the context of the Bohemian Reformation, "Antichrist" was a common term used to describe the perceived corruption of the medieval Papacy.</note>

**5.** But its lost light returned (by the gift of God and the benefit of the Reformation) with a new increase. As it was said in the promise (Isaiah 30:26): *the light of the Moon became as the light of the Sun*, through a better investigation of Truth from its deepest foundations than had ever been achieved in previous centuries.

**6.** However, because even the **Sun** of this heaven of ours (though nothing is brighter) *can and usually does fail* (Sirach 17:30 <note>original: *Eccl: 17. v. 30*; this refers to the Book of Sirach/Ecclesiasticus in the Vulgate Bible.</note>), and by God’s permission even the reformed Church suffers darkness once again, behold! The goodness and wisdom of the eternal Bridegroom gives hope that the Church will at last emerge as formidable to its enemies. He hints at how this will happen: namely (**Take Note**) if, setting aside distractions, the Church finally unites itself on all sides and connects itself with the Bonds of Order. In this way, like a well-prepared **Battle Array**, it shall be impenetrable to its enemies, while the Church itself shall penetrate, scatter, and overthrow whatever ranks are opposed to it.

**7.** And this is precisely what the Apostle urges in the letter to the Ephesians, and requires of the mature Church, saying: *Striving to preserve the* **UNITY** *of the Spirit in the bond of* **PEA-** <note>The text cuts off here at the end of the page with the catchword "CIS," which completes the Latin word *PACIS*, meaning "of Peace."</note>

<summary>Comenius outlines the four historical stages of the Church—Apostolic dawn, the era of heresy, the Reformation, and a future state of unified strength—arguing that the Church’s ultimate power lies in its internal order and unity.</summary>
<keywords>Apostolic Church, Heresy, Reformation, Antichrist, Church Unity, Song of Solomon, Order of the Church</keywords>

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the developmental stages of the Church. Having established the "Dawn" and "Moonlight" eras, Comenius now moves toward the goal of "Sunlight" and "Maturity," quoting heavily from the Apostle Paul to describe the Church as a growing body.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<header>is to grow.</header>
<sig>A 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 11. Can- (from Latin "Potest" or similar continuation)</meta>

...etc. and soon after: *Ascending on high he gave gifts to men, some indeed* **Apostles**, *some* **Prophets**, *others* **Evangelists**, *others* **Pastors** and **Teachers**: *for the perfecting of the saints, for the work of the ministry, for the edifying of the body of Christ*, **Until We All Come Into The Unity Of Faith, and the knowledge of the Son of God, Unto A Mature Man, To The Measure Of The Full Stature Of Christ**. *That we may no longer be* **Children**, *tossed to and fro and carried about by every wind of doctrine, by the cunning of men etc., but speaking the truth in love*, **May Grow Up Fully** *into him who is the Head, Christ. From whom* **The Whole Body, Being Fitly Joined Together** *and compacted, through every supporting joint, by the power acting within according to the measure of every member, takes its increase suitable for the Body, for its own building up, through love* (**Ephesians 4:3 etc.**) <note>original: *Ephes: 4. v. 3. &amp;c.* While Comenius cites verse 3, the passage he quotes actually spans verses 8 through 16 of Ephesians chapter 4.</note>

8. While we wait for this from divine goodness, why should we not reasonably investigate the nature of that Union and Order, in which such great power for the salvation of the Church is placed? And shouldn't we borrow from one another a participation and increase of Order, just as we do of Light? It is a pious and praiseworthy thing not to be found sluggish in this matter.

9. For me, since the opportunity has come to bring to light a new edition of the **Order of the Bohemian Brethren**, <note>original: *Fratrum Bohemorum Ordinem*. This refers to the *Ratio Disciplinae*, the governing constitution and ecclesiastical rules of the Unitas Fratrum, or Moravian Church.</note> it seems profitable to set before our eyes, by the example of this very Church, the steps through which God is accustomed to lead his people, or by which steps he perfects them. Whichever of the godly shall read these things, they will easily find occasions to consider the admirable course of divine Providence regarding themselves and their own Church.

10. God does all things beautifully, each thing in its own time, says Solomon (**Ecclesiastes 3:11**) implying that the Works of God always proceed by a certain order and by degrees, from the lesser to the greater. Certainly, the Work of Creation, the Work of Redemption, the Work of Sanctification, and the whole course of Divine Providence regarding the universe show this: especially (which we now take up for specific consideration) his admirable wisdom in always extending the tents of the Church into greater breadth or glory.

<summary>Comenius uses the writings of Paul and Solomon to argue that the Church is a living body designed by God to grow from infancy to maturity through structured order. He introduces his new edition of the Bohemian Brethren's "Order" as a practical template for this divine growth.</summary>

<keywords>Ephesians 4, maturity of the Church, Body of Christ, Bohemian Brethren, Church Order, Divine Providence, Ecclesiastes, Unitas Fratrum</keywords>

<page-num>4</page-num>
<header>->Of the Slavonic Churches<-</header>
<meta>catchword: in</meta>

11. The Son of God could indeed, having *entered* <note>original: *ingressus*</note> the world in human flesh, have made his Gospel known to the whole world by himself once the <term>mystery of Redemption</term> <note>The theological concept that Christ's death and resurrection achieved the salvation of humanity.</note> was complete. But it did not please him to do so: but rather to ordain certain men, Apostles, who going into the world would announce these treasures of divine grace to the nations: and these would entrust the same office to others, to succeed them; and these again to others, even to the end of the age. (Matthew 28:20. 2 Timothy 2:7. Titus 1:5.)

12. *For although the light of the Gospel was already scattered through the world by the Apostles themselves, even to the ends of the Earth (as the Scriptures speak and histories attest), yet that light in the first few centuries could be seen rather as sparks catching certain individuals and families here and there, than as the full light of nations.* Only in the fourth century (in the times of <term>Constantine the Great</term> <note>The Roman Emperor who issued the Edict of Milan in 313 AD, legalizing Christianity across the Roman Empire.</note>) did entire nations begin to be converted, and, having deserted paganism, openly profess Christianity. This in turn occurred first in the Roman Empire only, then elsewhere also, by a now perpetual progression through those twelve centuries, as one nation after another came into the Christian name, throughout Europe, Asia, Africa, and America.

13. This successive work of Divine providence also exerted itself most evidently in founding the Churches of the **Slavonic Nations** <note>original: *Slavonicarum Gentium*. Comenius uses this term to refer to the Slavic-speaking peoples of Central and Eastern Europe.</note> (who occupy the eastern side of Europe most extensively) and can be clearly seen: *among whom the sowing of the Gospel was already done in the time of the Apostles, yet only came to a full harvest eight centuries later, and gradually extended itself through them, now for the greater, now for the better;* intervening now and then (as usually happens with the <term>Moon</term> <note>A reference to the 'Moonlight' era of the church mentioned on the previous page, characterized by cycles of light and darkness.</note>) with decreases, spots, obscurations, and darkenings. I shall briefly provide the evidence for all of this: so that it may serve the occasion for still further progress toward the better, and finally settle into something that is best.

14. *That the sowing was done by the Apostles themselves to these Nations*, we have the Apostle of the Gentiles <note>The Apostle Paul.</note> as a witness: who writes to the Romans (15:19) that *from Jerusalem in a circuit he filled all places with the Gospel of Christ even as far as <term>Illyricum</term>.* <note>A Roman province covering parts of the modern-day Balkans, including Croatia, Albania, and Bosnia-Herzegovina.</note> And to Timothy he relates, *that Crescens had gone into Galatia, Titus into <term>Dalmatia</term>* (2 Timothy 4:10.) But the Illyrians and Dalmatians are equally a part of the Slavonic Nations, even unto this

<summary>Comenius describes how the Gospel was spread by human succession rather than divine decree, eventually reaching the Slavic peoples. He argues that while the seeds were sown by the Apostles Paul and Titus in regions like Illyricum and Dalmatia, the "full harvest" of Slavic Christianity did not arrive until eight centuries later.</summary>

### Sowing by the Apostles

<page-num>5</page-num>

->A 3<-

unto this day. That first sowing was not without fruit, as this shows: that *Jerome* <note>St. Jerome (c. 347–420 AD), though best known for the Latin Vulgate, was born in Dalmatia/Illyria and was claimed by later Slavic writers as one of their own.</note>, born in the city of *Stridon in Illyria*, intending to promote the growth of the Faith in his own nation, translated the books of the Holy Scriptures into the vernacular <term>idiom</term> <note>The common language spoken by ordinary people, rather than a formal language like Latin or Greek.</note>. And so, among the nations of Europe, the Slavs were the first to have the oracles of God entrusted to them in their native tongue. For those nations glory in this version to this very day, and use no other; although the speech of the people has now changed so much that the common folk do not understand that more ancient language in all its parts. Therefore, their Priests (**Ruthenians** <note>A historical term for East Slavic people, including ancestors of modern Ukrainians and Belarusians.</note> & **Muscovites** <note>People of the Grand Duchy of Moscow, ancestors of modern Russians.</note>) cultivate that ancient language through study, just as we do with Latin.

15. It can be gathered that the Churches were strongly increased throughout those nations from the sixth <term>Synod of Constantinople</term> <note>A high-level council of church leaders gathered to decide on matters of doctrine.</note>, celebrated in the year of Christ 680: where famous mention of the Slavs appears in these words; *Our humble proposal of the whole Council waited for all to help; lest if only a part knew what was being done, it might be hidden from the other part. Especially since in the midst of the Nations, both of the* LOMBARDS *as well as the* SLAVS, *many of our fellow-servants are known to be, who do not cease to be anxiously concerned about this matter, etc.* From this it is also clear that the Slavs, who were Christians at that time, shrunk back from the worship of images: since they had been afraid to attend that Synod, which was working in favor of Images.

16. Finally, in the second century thereafter, God opened the door of the Gospel to the whole Slavonic Nations on such an occasion. The BULGARIANS, a Slavic people, were exhausting the neighboring Empire of Constantinople with long wars: until the Emperor Michael III entered into a peace with them, in the year 845, after the sister of the Bulgarian King—whom the Greeks had captured in war—was returned. Because she had learned the Christian Religion while in captivity, she persuaded her brother to leave paganism and become a Christian: following whose example his subjects embraced the same faith. In this way, the Bulgarians *became the firstfruits of the Slavonic nations for Christ*: soon followed by other peoples of the same language throughout those regions (between the Danube, Greece, and Italy)—the Moesians, Rascians, Serbians, Bosnians, Croats, etc. Remarkable work was provided to them by *Cyril* and *Methodius* <note>Greek brothers and missionaries known as the "Apostles to the Slavs" who created the Glagolitic alphabet to translate the Bible.</note> (Greek Bishops, but also fluent in the Slavonic language)

# The Bohemian Church Founded by the Greek Church

<page-num>6</page-num>

<meta>catchword: elected,</meta>

...conferring. When they <note>Referring to the Greek missionaries Cyril and Methodius mentioned on the previous page.</note> had penetrated as far as *Moravia* (at that time called *Marcomannia* <note>original: "Marcomanniam"; a reference to the Marcomanni, a Germanic tribal confederation that previously occupied the region.</note> by the *Germans*) around the year 861, they also won over their King, *Svatopluk*, and shortly thereafter the Duke of the *Bohemians*, *Bořivoj*, to Christ. From there, the brilliance of the Gospel subsequently penetrated into *Poland* as well, in the year of Christ 965: seventy-one years after the conversion of the *Bohemians*; one hundred and four after the *Moravians*; and one hundred and twenty after the *Bulgarians*. Truly, the *Russians* and *Muscovites* (also *Slavic* peoples themselves) also passed entirely into the Christian name fifteen years later (in the year 980), after *Anna*, the sister of Emperor *Basil*, was given as a wife to *Vladimir*, the Duke of the *Kievans*.

17. From this it is clear that *all these Nations were converted by the help of the Eastern Church and initiated into Christianity with the Greek rite, including the Bohemian Church*. But the Roman Pope, who even then was an ambusher of the Churches of the World, did not cease to watch for opportunities to lead them under his own jurisdiction. What those opportunities were must be recounted in a few words—at least regarding the *Bohemians* for the time being.

18. Satan, the enemy of salvation, resenting these beginnings of salvation in *Bohemia*, incited his own agitators: through whom a portion of the Nobles and the common people, stubbornly adhering to Heathenism <note>original: "Gentilismo"</note>, were driven into a frenzy. First, they drove *Bořivoj* himself into exile. Then, after his grandson *Wenceslaus* was removed through martyrdom along with his grandmother *Ludmila*, they substituted *Boleslaus* <note>Boleslaus I, known as "the Cruel," who assassinated his brother Wenceslaus.</note>, who was still held captive by ethnic superstitions. Because of this, many of the Nobles, and indeed all the Priests, were either slaughtered, taken captive, or driven into exile, where they had scattered through the neighboring provinces of *Germany*; until Emperor *Otto* <note>Otto I, Holy Roman Emperor.</note>, arriving with an army, restrained their fury and compelled *Boleslaus* to restore the Temples, recall the exiles, and hand over his sons to be imbued with Christian sacred rites, in the year 940. Thus the *Bohemians*, having first been brought under the power of the Roman Emperor, soon also fell into the snares <note>original: "casses"; literally "hunting nets" or "snares."</note> of the Roman Bishop <note>The Pope.</note> by a new opportunity, which was as follows:

19. *Boleslaus the Pious* (the son of that cruel one), being very eager to propagate the Christian religion, founded a Bishopric at *Prague*, seeking a pastor for the new and weak flock, a man of great experience. Therefore, one was recommended, and elected,

# Captured by the Roman Church

<page-num>7</page-num>

<header>captured by Rome.</header>

<sig>A 4</sig>

<meta>catchword: of God</meta>

...elected, Ditmar the Saxon, a Canon of the recently founded Archbishopric of Magdeburg. Since it was necessary for him to be inaugurated, the Archbishop of Mainz <note>original: "Moguntinus"; the Archbishop of Mainz held significant power in the Holy Roman Empire.</note> was summoned: who, while prescribing the laws of a new People for the new Bishop, also (at the Pope's command) handed over the Roman Liturgies in the Latin language. The Bohemians were offended by this, and being unable to bear divine worship being performed in an unknown tongue, they were forced to seek the customary use of their mother tongue in sacred matters from Rome via Legates (Bolehost, Myslibor, etc.) in the year 977, and they obtained it. But one century later, Gregory VII (called Hildebrand) went to snatch it away from them, sending a prohibitory edict to Vratislaus, Duke of Bohemia, in the year 1079. This edict exists word-for-word in the *History of the Persecutions of the Bohemian Church* in Chapter 5. From that time, there was a perpetual struggle between the Bohemian Church and the Roman Church: the latter always threatening them with the yoke of its superstitions, the former always shaking it off, until this unhappy age of ours, in which it has succumbed. It will not be irrelevant to mention this briefly, at least the major points.

20. A full century later, when Pope Celestine sent the command for clerical celibacy (which had already been ordered elsewhere) to be thrust upon the Bohemians as well, his Legate (Cardinal Peter of Lavia) was nearly pelted with stones by the priests and elders of Prague, as the Papal writer Hájek <note>original: "Hageo"; referring to the chronicler Václav Hájek of Libočany.</note> testifies for the year 1197.

21. Another century having passed, the doctrine of Transubstantiation <note>The belief that the bread and wine of the Eucharist literally become the body and blood of Christ.</note> began to be thrust upon the churches, along with the sacrilegious communion of the Eucharist under only one kind <note>The practice of withholding the wine/chalice from the laypeople, reserved only for the priests.</note>. In Bohemia, however, this did not happen before the year 1361: when Charles IV, King of the Bohemians (and also Holy Roman Emperor), having changed the Bishopric into an Archbishopric and founded the University of Prague, summoned Masters and Doctors from Germany, France, and Italy. For these men began to strongly urge the custom of communicating under one kind, which had been accepted elsewhere: *the Bohemians again taking this innovation badly and strenuously opposing themselves to it.*

22. More fervent than the others here was a man of noble descent, Jan Milíč <note>original: "Johannes Milicius"; a precursor to Jan Hus known for his reformist preaching.</note>, a Canon of Prague and a preacher in the Cathedral of the Castle: *against so many open [transgressions]*
of God

<meta>This page continues the chronological account of the Bohemian Reformation's early pioneers. The previous page detailed the resistance of the Bohemian clergy against Roman mandates like the Latin liturgy and clerical celibacy in the 10th and 11th centuries.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>

<header>The Bohemian Church with the Roman Church</header>

...forcefully thundered <note>original: "detonans"</note> against the abuses creeping in against the Word of God, alongside his colleague, Conrad Stickna. When this became known, Pope Gregory XI sent a Bull, by which Milic and his followers <note>original: "Auditoribus"; literally "listeners" or "hearers," referring to his congregation or students.</note> were struck with anathema and handed over to the new Archbishop, Ernest of Pardubitz, to be punished. Consequently, the Archbishop threw Milic into prison; but, fearing the reaction of the common people, he released him and instead ordered him into exile in the year 1366.

23. His successor was Matthias of Janov, a native of Prague and the confessor to Emperor Charles IV. Undeterred by the fate of his predecessor, Matthias continued to most courageously defend <term>Communion under both kinds</term> <note>The practice of the laity receiving both the bread and the wine during the Eucharist, which became a central demand of the Bohemian reformers.</note>. He was also the one who authored the proposal and persuaded the Emperor to convoke a Council to remove the many creeping abuses. However, when the Emperor began to negotiate this matter with the Pope, the Pope was so incensed that he insisted on restraining these "reckless and heretical men." Charles, deluded by the authority of the Pontiff, ordered Matthias to leave the Kingdom, despite the affection he held for his confessor. Matthias later returned, but lived out the remainder of his life in private until the year 1394, when he passed away. It is recorded that when he was near death, he told his friends for their comfort: *The fury of the enemies of Truth has prevailed against us now, but it will not be forever. For a lowly people shall rise, without sword or power, against whom they will not be able to prevail.*

24. It is worth noting that the Bohemians were not only driven by the Spirit working from within, but were also spurred on by other pious men from elsewhere who recognized the "abomination of desolation" <note>A biblical reference (Matthew 24:15) often used by reformers to describe corruption within the Church.</note>. These sparks of purer Truth were brought back from the Universities of Paris and Oxford, where students traveled for the sake of their studies. This was especially true regarding the influence of John Wycliffe; I have included here a letter of his (written in 1387, the last year of his life, to Jan Hus, who was then still a young man), which I found among certain of our surviving antiquities.

<page-num>9</page-num>

<header>perpetual struggle.</header>

<sig>A 5</sig>

->Salvation, and if anything sweeter can be imagined, in the innermost heart <note>original: "visceribus"; literally "bowels" or "entrails," used biblically to denote the seat of deep affection and mercy.</note> of JESUS CHRIST.<-

Dearest Brothers in the Lord, whom I love in the truth—and not I alone, but also all who have known the truth. I speak of that truth which remains in us, and will be with us forever, through the grace of God. I rejoiced greatly when brothers came from you and bore witness to your Truth, showing that you also walk in the truth. I have heard, brother, how the **Antichrist** <note>In the context of the Bohemian Reformation, "Antichrist" was frequently used to refer to the perceived corruption within the Papacy rather than a single demonic figure.</note> saddens you, bringing many and various tribulations upon the faithful of Christ. Nor is it a wonder that such things happen among you, since throughout almost the entire world now the **Law of Christ** suffers oppression from Adversaries, and that great, red, many-headed **Dragon**, of whom John spoke in the **Apocalypse** <note>A reference to Revelation 12, where a dragon pursues a woman representing the Church.</note>, has cast out of his mouth a great flood after the woman to swallow her up. But the most faithful Lord will now surely rescue his one faithful bride. Let us therefore be strengthened in the Lord our God, and in His immense goodness, firmly believing that He will not permit His beloved ones to fail in their good purpose, if only we love Him with our whole heart (as we ought). For adversities would not prevail if iniquity did not prevail. Therefore, let no affliction or pressure for Christ cast us down: since we know that whomever the Lord receives as sons, He chastises. For the **Father of mercy** wishes us to be exercised by adversities in the present life, so that He may spare us afterward: because the gold which the supreme craftsman chooses, He wishes to be thoroughly purged by fire here, so that He may later store it in His most pure eternal treasury. We see the time we have here is short and transitory: but the life which we expect in the future is blessed and everlasting. Let us labor, therefore, as long as we have time, so that we may be found worthy to enter into that rest. What else, I ask, do we see in this Life, but pains and anguishes, and weariness, and—what ought to affect the faithful most—contempt and trampling underfoot of the divine Law. Let us strive, therefore, as much as we can, to lay hold of those goods which shall remain, and are eternal, having denied our transitory and falling senses. Let us look back upon the way of life— <note>The text cuts off mid-word; the catchword "tionem" completes "conversationem" or "conduct/way of life."</note>

<page-num>10</page-num>

<header>History</header>

<meta>catchword: that (original: "ut")</meta>

...conduct <note>original: "tionem," the conclusion of "conversationem," referring to the manner of living.</note> of the fathers of the former age, let us look at the Saints of both Testaments, how they in this sea endured tossings and adversities, prisons and chains; they were stoned, they were sawn asunder, they died by the edge of the sword. They went about in sheepskins and goatskins, and other things, which the **Epistle to the Hebrews** <note>A reference to the "Hall of Faith" in Hebrews 11, describing the suffering of ancient prophets.</note> recounts at length. All went along the narrow way by the footsteps of Christ, who said: *Where I am, there also my servant shall be*. Therefore, since we have so great a cloud of ancient holy witnesses surrounding us, let us lay aside, as much as in us lies, every weight and the sin that besets us, and let us run with patience the race set before us, looking to the author and finisher of our faith, Jesus, who for the joy set before him endured the cross, despising the shame. Let us reflect upon Him who endured such contradiction from sinners against Himself, so that we may not grow weary, losing heart. But let us seek help from the Lord with our heart, and fight manfully against his adversary, the **Antichrist** <term>Antichrist: In this context, a term used by reformers to describe the institutional corruption of the Church which they believed opposed the true teachings of Jesus.</term>. Let us love His law from the soul, and let us not be deceitful workers, but in all things, as much as the Lord grants, let us act confidently and be vigorous in the cause of God, in hope of eternal reward.

You, therefore, **Hus**, Brother most beloved in Christ—unknown to me indeed by face, but not in faith and love (for the ends of the Earth cannot tear apart those whom the love of Christ joins)—be strengthened in the grace which is given to you. Contend as a good soldier of Jesus Christ, in word and deed: and recall those whom you can to the way of truth: because neither on account of erroneous and lying decrees, nor on account of the errors of the Antichrist, must the truth of the **Gospel** be kept in silence. Rather, now that the cunning of Satan is weakened, comfort and strengthen the members of Christ. Because soon the Antichrist, by the will of the Lord, shall meet his end. This is a great joy to me, that in your Kingdom, and elsewhere, God has so strengthened the hearts of some that they endure prisons, exiles, and even deaths for the sake of the word of God with joy. What more I should write, most beloved, I do not know; but I confess that I would most gladly strengthen you, and all of you lovers of **Christ’s Law**, in that love of the Law of God. Therefore I salute them from the innermost heart <note>original: "visceribus"; literally "bowels" or "entrails," used biblically to denote the seat of deep affection and mercy.</note>; especially your companion in the Gospel of Christ, asking...

### Of Jan Hus

<page-num>11</page-num>

<header>Of Jan Hus</header>

<meta>catchword: im-</meta>

...that you may pray for me and the whole Church of Christ. Now may the God of Peace, who brought back from the dead the great shepherd of the sheep by the blood of the eternal Covenant, our Lord Jesus Christ, equip you in every good thing, so that you may do His will, performing in you what is pleasing in His sight, through Jesus Christ, to whom be glory forever and ever, Amen.

25. **Hus**, therefore, having been made a Professor of the University <term>University: Specifically the Charles University in Prague, the oldest in Central Europe.</term>, and later (in the year 1400) the Preacher of the University chapel called **Bethlehem**, took courage to attack those abominations now uncontrollably breaking in, and openly to unsheathe the sword of the Divine Word. He did this to the great applause of the people, not only in **Prague**, but also throughout the whole Kingdom. This was especially so since in the same year Master **Jerome of Prague**, returning from England, had brought the books of **Wycliffe** <note>John Wycliffe (c. 1320s–1384) was an English theologian whose calls for reform deeply influenced the Bohemian movement.</note> with him; Jerome used the same outspokenness <note>original: "parrhesia," a Greek term for bold or fearless speech.</note> in the School as Hus used in the Church to tear down errors. When the foreign members of the University—who prevailed in number and votes—opposed these pious attempts to purge the Church from the flood of superstitions and condemned the Articles of Wycliffe, the **Bohemians** challenged these champions of the Pope over the right of voting. They contended that this right belonged to them as natives, but not to the others as foreigners. When this dispute was brought before **King Wenceslaus** (the son of Charles), and he ruled in favor of the Bohemians, the angry Germans left Prague in droves. On this occasion, the Universities of **Leipzig** and **Erfurt** were founded. The Bohemians, however, elected Master Hus as Rector of the University by their votes in the year 1409.

26. The Monks, deserted by the King and the University, instigated the Archbishop and obtained a decree that (on July 16, 1410) Wycliffe’s teachings were again condemned and his books consigned to the flames. The University members avenged this disgrace with another disgrace. For when in the following year (1411) **Pope John** <note>This refers to John XXIII, now considered an "antipope," who reigned during the Western Schism.</note>, sounding the trumpet of war <note>original: "classicum canens," a Roman military metaphor for a signal to battle.</note> against the King of Naples, distributed **indulgences** <term>Indulgences: Certificates sold by the church intended to reduce the temporal punishment for sins; here, they were sold to fund a crusade against a political rival.</term> to those who would take up arms for the Church, and some of those peddlers of trifles <note>original: "nugivendis," a derogatory term for those selling religious trinkets or indulgences.</note> arrived in Prague, and that Crusade was proclaimed by the Monks from their pulpits, not only did some pious people from the common folk publicly protest against them, but the next day Master Hus proposed theses against the superstitious indulgences and publicly demonstrated their vanity. But when the former [the indulgence sellers] nevertheless persisted, a band of students...

<page-num>12</page-num>

<header>Bohemia</header>

<meta>catchword: (4) Ex-</meta>

Having made an assault, they snatched the [Papal] Bulls from him <note>The "Bulls" refers to the decrees issued by the Pope, likely those authorizing the sale of indulgences to fund a crusade against the King of Naples.</note>, and placed one from among their own number, dressed in the habit of a prostitute, upon a wagon. They adorned his chest with the Bulls, drove him around, and shouted: meanwhile, the "prostitute" attracted the surrounding crowd with the most alluring words and gestures, distributing mock "blessings." Finally, in the middle of the marketplace, they burned all the Bulls, along with many letters from the Pope and the prelates.

27. Irritated by all these things, the Pope summoned **Hus** to Rome; when he (at the dissuasion of the University and the Nobles) did not appear, the Pope (**John XXIII**) <note>John XXIII was an antipope during the Western Schism; he was later deposed by the same Council that condemned Hus.</note> sent an interdict <term>Interdict: An ecclesiastical censure that prohibited the celebration of Mass and other sacraments in a specific city or region.</term> against celebrating Mass in **Prague**, on account of the presence of the "stubborn transgressor" **Hus**. Seeing the magistrates raging, the common people split into opposing factions, and everything in a state of turmoil and agitation, **Hus** voluntarily left **Prague** and taught the Word of God from town to town, until the time when he was summoned to **Constance** to give an account of his doctrine, protected by the Emperor's letter of safe conduct. How they were received there by the assembly of the "Roman Synagogue" <note>original: "Romanæ Synagogæ Conciliabulo," a highly polemical term used by the author to disparage the Council of Constance as illegitimate or un-Christian.</note>, history knows: namely that in the year 1415, on July 6th, **Hus**, and in 1416, on May 30th, **Jerome**, were burned, and so forth. See the Martyrologies.

28. *The Works of Hus* were later published in **Nuremberg** in folio, in Latin. But **Otho Brunfels** <note>Otho Brunfels (1488–1534) was a German theologian and botanist who was an early supporter of the Reformation.</note> published, besides these, other manuscripts he had obtained in quarto, with a dedication directed to **Dr. Luther**. Namely:

1. *On the Anatomy of the Antichrist, one book.* <note>original: "De Anatomia Antichristi"</note>
2. *On the Mysteries of the Iniquity of the Antichrist.* <note>original: "De Mysteriis iniquitatis Antichristi"</note>
3. *On the Revelations of Christ and the Antichrist.*
4. *On the Abolishing of Sects and the Traditions of Men.*
5. *On the Unity of the Church, and Avoiding Schism.*
6. *On Evangelical Perfection.*
7. *On the Ruinous Nature of Human Traditions.*
8. *On the Kingdom, People, Life, and Manners of the Antichrist.*
9. *With several other fragments, such as:*
(1) On Avoiding Contention in Disputation.
(2) That the Apostles Labored with their Hands, content with very little.
(3) What it is to Depart from the Faith.

<summary>This page recounts the public mockery of Papal Bulls in Prague, Hus's exile and subsequent journey to the Council of Constance, and the eventual martyrdom of Hus and Jerome of Prague. It concludes with a bibliography of Hus's works published during the Reformation.</summary>

<page-num>13</page-num>

<header>Rebellion against the Pope.</header>

<meta>catchword: Sigismun-</meta>

(4) An explanation of that passage in Genesis 49: *Dan shall be a serpent in the way, etc.* <note>original: "Fiet Dan coluber &c."</note>
(5) Concerning the abomination of desolation.
(6) Concerning the Mystery of Joseph and Pharaoh.
(7) Concerning the comparison of wicked women with the Antichrist.

There also exist works in the Bohemian language which do not exist in Latin: such as the Postils <term>Postils: A collection of sermons or commentaries on the Gospel readings for the Church year.</term> on the Sunday Gospels, full of Spirit and Zeal against all kinds of corruption among the Christian people, etc.

29. Indignant at the Pope’s savagery in treating his scholars (contrary to the pledge of safety given to them), the **Nobles of Bohemia and Moravia**, in the name of the whole community, sent letters to the Council. These were signed by hand and had seals attached all around them, protesting that **their Pastor, an innocent man, pious, holy, and a faithful Teacher of the truth, had been unjustly condemned.** These were sent from **Prague** on September 21, 1416. (I have witnesses worthy of belief that the original authentic copy of these letters is preserved to this day in the Library of the University of Aberdeen). The Synod <note>The Council of Constance.</note> did not reply to them; instead, they wrote to others whom they knew to be still deluded by Roman superstitions and placed in positions of power (specifically **John of Michelsberg, Alseus Skopek of Duba, Albert of Kolditz, etc.**). They prayed and pleaded that these men would hold the Roman Catholic Church in high regard and assist the Council’s legate, **John, Bishop of Litomyšl**, in crushing the heresy. These letters were sent from **Constance** on March 22, 1417.

30. Thus, with the Bohemians armed by the Council for mutual discord, contentions, brawls, and hatreds were sharpened more and more every day. The petty priests <note>original: "sacrificuli" - a derogatory term used by the author to describe Roman Catholic priests as mere "sacrificers" rather than true ministers.</note> proclaimed excommunication against the **Hussites** from their pulpits and cursed them with dire oaths. They also persecuted them, seizing their churches by force wherever they could. This situation caused such a riot in **Prague** on July 30, 1419, that the incensed commoners threw twelve Senators of Old Prague, along with the city mayor, through the windows of the town hall, where they were caught on the points of spears. <note>This event is known as the First Defenestration of Prague, a pivotal moment of violence marking the start of the Hussite Wars.</note>

31. Soon, however, after **King Wenceslaus** died without an heir,
